They were politically faithful to their father throughout his life, but after 1945 each drifted away from the Liberal Party, Gwilym finishing his career as <a href="/wiki/Home_Secretary" title="Home Secretary">Home Secretary</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Conservative_Party_(UK)" title="Conservative Party (UK)">Conservatives</a> in the 1950s and Megan becoming a Labour MP in 1957.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Frances">Frances</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=61" title="Edit section: Frances">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Lloyd George met <a href="/wiki/Frances_Stevenson" title="Frances Stevenson">Frances Stevenson</a> in 1910; she worked for him first as a teacher for Megan in 1911;<sup id="cite_ref-LongfordGreater_217-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LongfordGreater-217">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 1">&#58;&#8202;1&#8202;</span></sup> she became his secretary and, from early 1913, his long-term mistress.<sup id="cite_ref-LongfordGreater_217-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LongfordGreater-217">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 11–12">&#58;&#8202;11–12&#8202;</span></sup> Lloyd George may have been the father of Stevenson's daughter Jennifer (1929–2012), born long before they wed,<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup> but it is more likely that she was the daughter of <a href="/wiki/Thomas_F._Tweed" title="Thomas F. Tweed">Thomas Tweed</a>, with whom Stevenson had had an affair.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> To the disapproval of his children he finally married Frances in October 1943; he was aged 80 at the time.<sup id="cite_ref-LongfordAWar_220-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LongfordAWar-220">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 154–156">&#58;&#8202;154–156&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>Frances was the first Countess Lloyd-George, and is now largely remembered for her diaries, which dealt with the great issues, and statesmen, of Lloyd George's heyday. A volume of their letters, <i>My Darling Pussy</i>, has also been published; Lloyd George's nickname for Frances referred to her gentle personality.<sup id="cite_ref-LongfordGreater_217-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LongfordGreater-217">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 12">&#58;&#8202;12&#8202;</span></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Womanising">Womanising</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=62" title="Edit section: Womanising">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Lloyd George had a considerable reputation as a womaniser, including an alleged long affair with the wife of a Parliamentary colleague in the 1890s. His biographer Travis Crosbie comments that although he clearly enjoyed the company of women much of the information is based on hearsay rather than actual evidence and that his reputation may well be considerably exaggerated.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECrosby2014ch._To_England_To_Parliament,_pp._40–41_221-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrosby2014ch._To_England_To_Parliament,_pp._40–41-221">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Descendants">Descendants</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=63" title="Edit section: Descendants">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Canadian historian <a href="/wiki/Margaret_MacMillan" title="Margaret MacMillan">Margaret MacMillan</a>, who detailed Lloyd George's role at the 1919 Peace Conference in her book, <i><a href="/wiki/Peacemakers:_The_Paris_Peace_Conference_of_1919_and_Its_Attempt_to_End_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Peacemakers: The Paris Peace Conference of 1919 and Its Attempt to End War">Peacemakers</a></i>, is his great-granddaughter. The British television historian and presenter <a href="/wiki/Dan_Snow" title="Dan Snow">Dan Snow</a> is a great-great-grandson through his mother, Canadian-born Ann MacMillan (married <a href="/wiki/Peter_Snow" title="Peter Snow">Peter Snow</a>), a long-time <a href="/wiki/Canadian_Broadcasting_Corporation" title="Canadian Broadcasting Corporation">CBC</a> reporter based in London and sister of Margaret MacMillan.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Honours">Honours</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=64" title="Edit section: Honours">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Arms_of_Lloyd_George,_Earl_Lloyd-George_of_Dwyfor.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Arms_of_Lloyd_George%2C_Earl_Lloyd-George_of_Dwyfor.svg/130px-Arms_of_Lloyd_George%2C_Earl_Lloyd-George_of_Dwyfor.svg.png" decoding="async" width="130" height="157" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Arms_of_Lloyd_George%2C_Earl_Lloyd-George_of_Dwyfor.svg/195px-Arms_of_Lloyd_George%2C_Earl_Lloyd-George_of_Dwyfor.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Arms_of_Lloyd_George%2C_Earl_Lloyd-George_of_Dwyfor.svg/260px-Arms_of_Lloyd_George%2C_Earl_Lloyd-George_of_Dwyfor.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="617" /></a><figcaption><i>Lloyd George <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms" title="Coat of arms">arms</a></i></figcaption></figure>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Decorations">Decorations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=66" title="Edit section: Decorations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Gal%C3%B3_de_l%27Orde_del_M%C3%A8rit_(UK).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Gal%C3%B3_de_l%27Orde_del_M%C3%A8rit_%28UK%29.svg/60px-Gal%C3%B3_de_l%27Orde_del_M%C3%A8rit_%28UK%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="17" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Gal%C3%B3_de_l%27Orde_del_M%C3%A8rit_%28UK%29.svg/90px-Gal%C3%B3_de_l%27Orde_del_M%C3%A8rit_%28UK%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Gal%C3%B3_de_l%27Orde_del_M%C3%A8rit_%28UK%29.svg/120px-Gal%C3%B3_de_l%27Orde_del_M%C3%A8rit_%28UK%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="218" data-file-height="60" /></a></span> <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Merit" title="Order of Merit">Order of Merit</a> (Civil) 1919<sup id="cite_ref-WhoLloydGeorge_223-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WhoLloydGeorge-223">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Order_of_St_John_(UK)_ribbon_-vector.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Order_of_St_John_%28UK%29_ribbon_-vector.svg/60px-Order_of_St_John_%28UK%29_ribbon_-vector.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="17" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Order_of_St_John_%28UK%29_ribbon_-vector.svg/90px-Order_of_St_John_%28UK%29_ribbon_-vector.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Order_of_St_John_%28UK%29_ribbon_-vector.svg/120px-Order_of_St_John_%28UK%29_ribbon_-vector.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="442" data-file-height="126" /></a></span> Knight of Grace, <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Saint_John_(chartered_1888)" title="Order of Saint John (chartered 1888)">Order of Saint John</a>; Chancellor of the Welsh Priory from 1918 and Prior of Wales from 1943.<sup id="cite_ref-bpeerage_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bpeerage-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup></li>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Academic">Academic</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=67" title="Edit section: Academic">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Oxford_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Oxford University">Oxford University</a> – <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_Civil_Law" title="Doctor of Civil Law">DCL</a> 1908<sup id="cite_ref-WhoLloydGeorge_223-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WhoLloydGeorge-223">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Fellow" title="Fellow">Fellow</a> of <a href="/wiki/Jesus_College,_Oxford" title="Jesus College, Oxford">Jesus College</a> 1910</li></ul></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/University_of_Wales" title="University of Wales">University of Wales</a> – <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_Laws" class="mw-redirect" title="Doctor of Laws">LLD</a> 1908<sup id="cite_ref-WhoLloydGeorge_223-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WhoLloydGeorge-223">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Glasgow_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Glasgow University">Glasgow University</a> – LLD 1917<sup id="cite_ref-bpeerage_16-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bpeerage-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/University_of_Edinburgh" title="University of Edinburgh">University of Edinburgh</a> – LLD 1918<sup id="cite_ref-WhoLloydGeorge_223-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WhoLloydGeorge-223">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Rector_of_the_University_of_Edinburgh" title="Rector of the University of Edinburgh">Rector</a> – 1920<sup id="cite_ref-bpeerage_16-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bpeerage-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup></li></ul></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Durham_University" title="Durham University">Durham University</a> – DCL 1919<sup id="cite_ref-bpeerage_16-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bpeerage-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Sheffield_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Sheffield University">Sheffield University</a> – <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_Letters" title="Doctor of Letters">DLitt</a> 1919<sup id="cite_ref-WhoLloydGeorge_223-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WhoLloydGeorge-223">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Cambridge_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Cambridge University">Cambridge University</a> – LLD 1920<sup id="cite_ref-bpeerage_16-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bpeerage-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup></li>

<li><a href="/wiki/Leeds_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Leeds University">Leeds University</a> – LLD 1922<sup id="cite_ref-bpeerage_16-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bpeerage-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Freedoms">Freedoms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=68" title="Edit section: Freedoms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<p>Lloyd George was made <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_the_City" title="Freedom of the City">Honorary Freeman</a> of the following cities and towns:<sup id="cite_ref-bpeerage_16-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bpeerage-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Cardiff" title="Cardiff">Cardiff</a> – 24 June 1908<sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/City_of_London" title="City of London">City of London</a> – 1908
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Livery_company#governance" title="Livery company">Master</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Worshipful_Company_of_Curriers" title="Worshipful Company of Curriers">Worshipful Company of Curriers</a> (London)</li></ul></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Birmingham" title="Birmingham">Birmingham</a>, Manchester – 1908</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Blackpool" title="Blackpool">Blackpool</a> – 1918<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Neath" title="Neath">Neath</a> – 1920<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Criccieth" title="Criccieth">Criccieth</a> – July 1919.<sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Bristol" title="Bristol">Bristol</a>, <a href="/wiki/York" title="York">York</a>, Glasgow, <a href="/wiki/Barnsley" title="Barnsley">Barnsley</a> – 1921</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Leeds" title="Leeds">Leeds</a>, <a href="/wiki/Aberystwyth" title="Aberystwyth">Aberystwyth</a><sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> – 1922</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/City_of_Salford" title="City of Salford">Salford</a> – October 1922<sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Montreal" title="Montreal">Montreal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brecon" title="Brecon">Brecon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Llandovery" title="Llandovery">Llandovery</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carmarthen" title="Carmarthen">Carmarthen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Llanelli" title="Llanelli">Llanelli</a>, <a href="/wiki/Swansea" title="Swansea">Swansea</a> – 1923</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Portsmouth" title="Portsmouth">Portsmouth</a> – 1924<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Namesakes">Namesakes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=69" title="Edit section: Namesakes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Lloyd_George_Avenue" title="Lloyd George Avenue">Lloyd George Avenue</a> is an extension of the <a href="/wiki/A470" class="mw-redirect" title="A470">A470</a> road, connecting Central <a href="/wiki/Cardiff" title="Cardiff">Cardiff</a> to <a href="/wiki/Cardiff_Bay" title="Cardiff Bay">Cardiff Bay</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mount_Lloyd_George" title="Mount Lloyd George">Mount Lloyd George</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Rocky_Mountains" title="Northern Rocky Mountains">Northern Rocky Mountains</a> of <a href="/wiki/British_Columbia" title="British Columbia">British Columbia</a>, Canada was named after Lloyd George during the First World War, and still retains the name.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAkriggAkrigg1997155_232-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAkriggAkrigg1997155-232">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Kibbutz" title="Kibbutz">Kibbutz</a> <a href="/wiki/Ramat_David" title="Ramat David">Ramat David</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Jezreel_Valley" title="Jezreel Valley">Jezreel Valley</a> in northern Israel and the adjacent <a href="/wiki/Ramat_David_Airbase" title="Ramat David Airbase">Ramat David Airbase</a> are named after him.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/David_Lloyd_George_Elementary_School" class="mw-redirect" title="David Lloyd George Elementary School">David Lloyd George Elementary School</a> in Vancouver was named after Lloyd George in 1921.<sup id="cite_ref-233" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-233">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Cultural_depictions">Cultural depictions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=70" title="Edit section: Cultural depictions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Selected_works">Selected works</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=71" title="Edit section: Selected works">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/peoplesbudget00lloy/page/n3/mode/2up">The People's Budget</a></i>, Hodder &amp; Stoughton, 1909</li>
<li><i>The Lords, The Land and the People</i>, Hodder &amp; Stoughton, 1909</li>
<li><i>The People's Will</i>, Hodder &amp; Stoughton, 1910</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/bettertimes00lloyrich/page/n7/mode/2up">Better Times</a></i>, Hodder &amp; Stoughton, 1910</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/peoplesinsurance00lloyrich/page/n7/mode/2up">The People's Insurance</a></i>, Hodder &amp; Stoughton, 1912</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/throughterrortot00lloyuoft/mode/2up">Through Terror to Triumph</a></i> (edited by <a href="/wiki/Frances_Stevenson" title="Frances Stevenson">Frances Stevenson</a>), Hodder and Stoughton, 1915</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.38082">The Great Crusade</a></i> (edited by Frances Stevenson), Hodder and Stoughton, 1918</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.81644">Is It Peace?</a></i>, Hodder and Stoughton, 1923</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/wherearewegoing00lloy/page/n5/mode/2up">Where Are We Going?</a></i>, George H. Doran Company, 1923 (American version of <i>Is It Peace?</i>, same contents but re-arranged)</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/dli.csl.6228/mode/2up">Slings and Arrows</a></i> (selected and with an introduction by <a href="/wiki/Philip_Guedalla" title="Philip Guedalla">Philip Guedalla</a>), Cassell and Company, Ltd, 1929</li>
<li><i>How to Tackle Unemployment</i> (with <a href="/wiki/Philip_Kerr,_11th_Marquess_of_Lothian" title="Philip Kerr, 11th Marquess of Lothian">the Marquess of Lothian</a> and <a href="/wiki/B._Seebohm_Rowntree" class="mw-redirect" title="B. Seebohm Rowntree">B. Seebohm Rowntree</a>), The Press Printers Ltd, 1930</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.92942">The Truth About Reparations and War-Debts</a></i>, William Heinemann Ltd, 1932</li>
<li><i>War Memoirs</i>, 6 volumes, Ivor Nicholson and Watson, 1933 – 1936: re-published in 2 volumes by Odhams Press, 1938</li>
<li><i>Organizing Prosperity</i>, Ivor Nicholson and Watson, 1935</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.57897">The Truth About the Peace Treaties Volume I</a></i>, Victor Gollancz Ltd, 1938</li>
<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/dli.ernet.527901">The Truth About the Peace Treaties Volume II</a></i>, Victor Gollancz Ltd, 1938
<ul><li>Published in the US as <i>Memoirs of the Peace Conference</i>, 2 volumes, <a href="/wiki/Yale_University_Press" title="Yale University Press">Yale University Press</a>, 1939</li></ul></li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=David_Lloyd_George&amp;action=edit&amp;section=73" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-43"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-43">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Strictly speaking, the Welsh dragon is actually <i>passant</i>. The dragon on the Tudor coat of arms was <i>rampant</i>. See <a href="/wiki/Attitude_(heraldry)" title="Attitude (heraldry)">Attitude (heraldry)</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-51"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-51">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Scotland had, and has, its own education system, separate from that of England and Wales.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
